=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a tree with N vertices. The i-th edge (1 \leq i \leq N-1) connects vertices u_i and v_i bidirectionally.
Adding one undirected edge to the given tree always yields a graph with exactly one cycle.
Among such graphs, how many satisfy all of the following conditions?

- The graph is simple.
- All vertices in the cycle have degree 3.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
u_1 v_1
u_2 v_2
\vdots
u_{N-1} v_{N-1}

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 3 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq u_i, v_i \leq N
- The given graph is a tree.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

6
1 2
2 3
3 4
4 5
3 6

Sample Output 1

1

Adding an edge connecting vertices 2 and 4 yields a simple graph where all vertices in the cycle have degree 3, so it satisfies the conditions.

Sample Input 2

7
1 2
2 7
3 5
7 3
6 2
4 7

Sample Output 2

0

There are cases where no graphs satisfy the conditions.

Sample Input 3

15
1 15
11 14
2 10
1 7
9 8
6 9
4 12
14 5
4 9
8 11
7 4
1 13
3 6
11 10

Sample Output 3

6

```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Thus algorithm:
1. Read N, adjacency list.
2. Compute deg array.
3. Mark isB = deg == 3; isA = deg == 2.
4. Need traverse components of B. We can use visitedB boolean.
For each B vertex not visited:
   - BFS/DFS stack over B vertices connected via edges where both endpoints B.
   - cntA = 0
   - For each B vertex popped, for each neighbor nb:
       if deg[nb] == 2: cntA += 1
       elif deg[nb] == 3 and not visitedB[nb]: mark and push
   - ans += cntA * (cntA - 1) // 2
But careful: When iterating neighbors of B, if neighbor is B and not visited, push. If neighbor is B already visited, skip. If neighbor deg2, count. If neighbor deg1 or deg>=4, ignore. Implementation details:
```
import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N = int(input())
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    for _ in range(N-1):
        u,v = map(int, input().split())
        u-=1; v-=1
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)
    deg = [len(adj[i]) for i in range(N)]
    visited = [False]*N
    ans = 0
    for i in range(N):
        if deg[i] == 3 and not visited[i]:
            stack = [i]
            visited[i] = True
            cntA = 0
            while stack:
                v = stack.pop()
                for to in adj[v]:
                    if deg[to] == 2:
                        cntA += 1
                    elif deg[to] == 3 and not visited[to]:
                        visited[to] = True
                        stack.append(to)
            ans += cntA * (cntA - 1) // 2
    print(ans)
if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Code:
```
def solve():
    import sys
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    idx = 1
    for _ in range(N-1):
        u = data[idx] - 1
        v = data[idx+1] - 1
        idx += 2
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)
    deg = [len(adj[i]) for i in range(N)]
    visited = bytearray(N)
    ans = 0
    for i in range(N):
        if deg[i] == 3 and not visited[i]:
            stack = [i]
            visited[i] = 1
            cntA = 0
            while stack:
                v = stack.pop()
                for to in adj[v]:
                    dt = deg[to]
                    if dt == 2:
                        cntA += 1
                    elif dt == 3 and not visited[to]:
                        visited[to] = 1
                        stack.append(to)
            ans += cntA * (cntA - 1) // 2
    print(ans)
```

Could avoid data list by reading line by line to reduce memory, but fine. 4e5 ints ~11 MB, adj ~ maybe 20 MB, total okay under typical 256 MB? Python int overhead large: data list of 400k Python ints ~11.2 MB (28 bytes each) plus list pointers ~3.2 MB, total ~14 MB. Adj list: 2e5 empty lists ~56 bytes each = 11.2 MB, plus 400k ints references in lists ~3.2 MB, but ints are existing? Actually when appending u/v, Python ints from data are reused? We compute u = data[idx]-1 creates new int objects, append references. Each edge stores two ints, so 400k int objects? Wait u and v are Python ints; when we do u = data[idx]-1, it creates a new int object for u, and v similarly. Appending stores reference to that int object. For each edge, two int objects created and stored in adj. So 400k int objects ~11.2 MB, plus list pointers ~3.2 MB, plus list overhead ~11.2 MB, plus data list ~14 MB, total ~40 MB. Still okay for 2e5. But to be memory efficient, we can read line by line and not store data list. Let's use `sys.stdin.buffer.readline` to avoid data list. Simpler:
```
import sys
input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
N = int(input())
adj = [[] for _ in range(N)]
for _ in range(N-1):
    u,v = map(int, input().split())
...
```
This is fine for 2e5. Use buffer.readline. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    for _ in range(N - 1):
        u, v = map(int, input().split())
        u -= 1
        v -= 1
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)

    deg = [len(adj[i]) for i in range(N)]
    visited = bytearray(N)
    ans = 0

    for i in range(N):
        if deg[i] == 3 and not visited[i]:
            stack = [i]
            visited[i] = 1
            cnt_deg2 = 0

            while stack:
                v = stack.pop()
                for to in adj[v]:
                    if deg[to] == 2:
                        cnt_deg2 += 1
                    elif deg[to] == 3 and not visited[to]:
                        visited[to] = 1
                        stack.append(to)

            ans += cnt_deg2 * (cnt_deg2 - 1) // 2

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
